eng-g02-t03-u10-cq013 · unit 10 · Short answer · The My English World Project Brief

Is ‘The My English World Project Brief’ a story or a fact text? How can you tell?

answer: A fact text: it gives instructions in four numbered parts.

The brief is not made up and has no story events. It says it “has four parts, and you work through them in order”, and each part starts with a label - Part 1: Choose your pages. That is how instructions are built.

the text it is asked about
My English World is your last project of Year 2, and Teacher Yasmin gives every class the same brief on the first Monday of the term. It has four parts, and you work through them in order, just like Amal did with her own booklet.

Part 1: Choose your pages. Pick six pages from at least four different units. Look for work that shows reading, writing, speaking and new words. Amal chose a page about a helpful neighbour, a page about the parts of the body, a page about the sun and shadows, and a page about the library in the city. She read through her old work first, so she picked her strongest pages instead of her newest ones.

Part 2: Make the booklet. Write two full sentences on every page, add one picture, and add one label. A label can be a single word, like butterfly or shadow, pointing at the exact part of your picture it describes. Keep your sentences simple and clear, the way you have all year: one main idea in every sentence, and a full stop at the end.

Part 3: Present your work. Speak for about two minutes at the class showcase. Point at your pictures as you talk, and answer one question from a listener. Practise out loud three times before showcase day, even if nobody else is in the room. Your own voice, heard again and again, is one of the best teachers you have.

Part 4: Reflect. Write three sentences about what you can do now that you could not do at the start of Year 2. Then write one goal for Year 3, such as reading a longer book or writing a longer story.

Read every page aloud before you hand it in. Your ears will find the missing full stops faster than your eyes will. If a partner or an adult is nearby, ask for one kind idea and one careful question about your work; if nobody is free, read it aloud to yourself instead and listen for any sentence that sounds unfinished. Fix what needs fixing, and keep the pages that are already strong. When your booklet is ready, you will be ready too.

eng-g02-t03-u10-cq006 · unit 10 · Short answer · The My English World Project Brief

How many parts does the My English World project have, and what happens in Part 3?

answer: It has four parts. In Part 3 you present your work for about two minutes.

The brief lists Part 1 to Part 4, and Part 3 is the two-minute presentation.

eng-g02-t03-u10-cq010 · unit 10 · Short answer · Showcase Day

Which page is Amal most proud of, and which two things does she say it shows?

answer: Page four, because it shows the sun and her long shadow.

Amal answers Teacher Yasmin's question by naming page four, the sun and the long shadow.

the text it is asked about
Teacher Yasmin: Welcome, everybody, to our end-of-year showcase. A whole year of work fills this hall today, so take your time and look closely. Amal, would you like to present first?
Amal: Yes, please. Hello, my name is Amal, and my booklet is called My English World.
Teacher Yasmin: Lovely. Which page are you most proud of, Amal?
Amal: Page four. It shows the sun and my long shadow. Unit 4 taught me why shadows grow longer in the evening, so I drew mine at three different times of day.
Nora: I like your bug page too. The butterfly has beautiful wings.
Amal: Thank you, Nora. I labelled the wings and the antennae, and I coloured every wing a different pattern.
Theo: What about your neighbour page? I remember you asked me two questions for it.
Amal: I did! You told me you water our plants when we are away, so I used that as my example sentence.
Theo: I am glad it made the final six pages.
Leo: How did you make the plant page better?
Amal: I read it aloud to Adam. He said it was too short, so I added two facts about planting.
Teacher Yasmin: That is careful work, Amal. It shows real listening, and real revising. What is your goal for next year?
Amal: I want to read a long chapter book by myself, from the first page to the last.
Teacher Yasmin: A wonderful goal. Thank you, Amal. Nora, would you like to present next?
Nora: Yes, Teacher Yasmin. My booklet is called Nora's Word Journey, and my favourite page is the library page.
Teacher Yasmin: Wonderful. Everyone, please clap for Amal before we continue.
Teacher Yasmin: Thank you all for listening so carefully today. Remember, a good audience asks kind questions and waits for a full answer, just like Nora, Theo and Leo did for Amal.

eng-g02-t03-u10-cq011 · unit 10 · Short answer · Showcase Day

How did Amal make her plant page better after she read it to Adam?

answer: Adam said it was too short, so she added two facts about planting.

Amal tells Leo exactly what feedback she was given and what she added because of it.

Unit 1 — Welcome and Calendar

eng-g02-t01-u01-quiz01-q01 · unit 1 · Multiple choice · Welcome and Calendar checkpoint

What does the word name mean?

A person you work or play with.  |  The word that people call you.  |  A chart that shows the months.  |  To enjoy something very much.

A name is what people call you. The other three choices describe a partner, a calendar and the verb like.

eng-g02-t01-u01-quiz01-q02 · unit 1 · Multiple choice · Welcome and Calendar checkpoint

Can you ____ your name for me, please?

spell  |  brown  |  eleventh  |  calendar

Spell means to say or write the letters in order. Brown is a colour, eleventh is an order word and a calendar is a chart.

eng-g02-t01-u01-quiz01-q03 · unit 1 · Multiple choice · Welcome and Calendar checkpoint

Which word means a person you work or play with?

picture  |  week  |  partner  |  yellow

A partner works or plays with you. A picture is a drawing, a week is seven days and yellow is a colour.

eng-g02-t01-u01-quiz01-q04 · unit 1 · Multiple choice · Welcome and Calendar checkpoint

This is Nora. ____ likes singing.

He  |  She  |  They  |  We

Nora is one girl, so we use she. He is for a boy, and they and we are used for more than one person.

eng-g02-t01-u01-quiz01-q05 · unit 1 · Multiple choice · Welcome and Calendar checkpoint

Leo ____ football and mangoes.

like  |  liking  |  liked  |  likes

Leo is one person, just like he, so the verb needs the s. Like belongs with I, you, we and they.

eng-g02-t01-u01-quiz01-q06 · unit 1 · Multiple choice · Welcome and Calendar checkpoint

Which day comes straight after Wednesday?

Monday  |  Friday  |  Tuesday  |  Thursday

The order in the song is Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday. Monday, Friday and Tuesday sit in other places in the week.

eng-g02-t01-u01-quiz01-q07 · unit 1 · Multiple choice · Welcome and Calendar checkpoint

How many months are there in one year?

7  |  12  |  30  |  10

A year has twelve months. Seven is the number of days in a week, thirty is the number of days in many months, and ten is the number of your fingers.

eng-g02-t01-u01-quiz01-q08 · unit 1 · Multiple choice · Welcome and Calendar checkpoint

Which word is the order word for number 3?

three  |  thirty  |  third  |  Thursday

Third tells you the position in a line or on a calendar. Three and thirty are counting numbers, and Thursday is a day.

eng-g02-t01-u01-quiz01-q09 · unit 1 · Multiple choice · Welcome and Calendar checkpoint

In the story, what did Amal do when Nora stood shyly by the door?

She asked Nora to be her partner.  |  She read the calendar to the class.  |  She drew a picture of a clever fox.  |  She waved goodbye and went home.

Amal remembered how kind Leo had been on her own first day, so she welcomed Nora and offered to be her partner.

eng-g02-t01-u01-quiz01-q10 · unit 1 · Multiple choice · Welcome and Calendar checkpoint

Which one is a complete question about colour?

What colour is your bag?  |  Your bag colour is.  |  Colour bag what is?  |  How colour your bag.

The pattern is “What colour is ...?” with a question mark at the end. The other three do not use the question words in the right order.

Unit 2 — Good Neighbours and Jobs

eng-g02-t01-u02-quiz01-q01 · unit 2 · Multiple choice · Good Neighbours and Jobs checkpoint

What does the word neighbour mean?

A person who puts out fires and rescues people.  |  A person who owns a shop and sells things.  |  A person who lives near you.  |  A hard hat that protects the head.

A neighbour lives close to your home. The other options describe a firefighter, a shopkeeper and a helmet.

eng-g02-t01-u02-quiz01-q02 · unit 2 · Cloze · Good Neighbours and Jobs checkpoint

The ______ uses a long pole to reach the high windows.

window cleaner  |  helmet  |  uniform  |  busy market square

Only a person can use a pole. A helmet and a uniform are clothing, and a market square is a place.

eng-g02-t01-u02-quiz01-q03 · unit 2 · Multiple choice · Good Neighbours and Jobs checkpoint

Which piece of equipment helps a firefighter breathe fresh air inside smoke?

A badge  |  A tractor  |  A whistle  |  A mask

The mask covers the nose and mouth. A badge and a whistle belong to a police officer, and a tractor belongs to a farmer.

eng-g02-t01-u02-quiz01-q04 · unit 2 · Multiple choice · Good Neighbours and Jobs checkpoint

Which of these questions asks about an action?

Who is the bus driver?  |  What is the police officer doing?  |  Who is the firefighter helping today?  |  Who is your teacher?

An action is a thing and not a person, so it takes What. The other three questions all ask about a person.

eng-g02-t01-u02-quiz01-q05 · unit 2 · Multiple choice · Good Neighbours and Jobs checkpoint

The firefighters ______ climbing the ladder.

am  |  is  |  are  |  be

Firefighters means more than one person, so the helping word is are. Am belongs with I, and is belongs with one person.

eng-g02-t01-u02-quiz01-q06 · unit 2 · Multiple choice · Good Neighbours and Jobs checkpoint

Which is the correct -ing form of the verb drive?

driving  |  driveing  |  drived  |  drivving

Drive ends in a silent e, so the e is dropped before -ing. Drived is not a word, and the v is never doubled.

eng-g02-t01-u02-quiz01-q07 · unit 2 · Multiple choice · Good Neighbours and Jobs checkpoint

In the story, how many passengers does Amal count on Nadia's bus?

Two  |  Five  |  Seven  |  Ten

Amal counts all the way from one to ten, then says that there are ten people on the bus.

eng-g02-t01-u02-quiz01-q08 · unit 2 · Multiple choice · Good Neighbours and Jobs checkpoint

In the story, what does Karim use to reach the high glass?

A ladder and a rope  |  A long pole  |  A hose and a bucket of water  |  A tractor and some seeds

The story says Karim used a long pole. A hose belongs to the firefighters, and a tractor belongs to a farmer.

eng-g02-t01-u02-quiz01-q09 · unit 2 · Cloze · Good Neighbours and Jobs checkpoint

The bus driver wears a blue ______ to work every day.

helmet  |  tractor  |  uniform  |  stethoscope

A uniform is the set of clothes worn for a job. A helmet is worn by firefighters, and a tractor and a stethoscope are not clothes.

eng-g02-t01-u02-quiz01-q10 · unit 2 · Multiple choice · Good Neighbours and Jobs checkpoint

What does the word rescuing mean?

Selling bread and fruit in a small shop  |  Making plants get bigger with water and sun  |  Cleaning the high windows with a long pole  |  Saving someone from danger

Rescuing means bringing someone out of danger. The other options describe a shopkeeper, a farmer and a window cleaner.

Unit 3 — Ready, Steady, Go!

eng-g02-t01-u03-predq1 · unit 3 · Short answer · The Big Race

Before the last part of The Big Race, Amal stops to help Theo up. What did that tell you about how the race would end for her?

That she would be called the winner for being kind, not for being first  |  That she would come first and win the cup  |  That she would give up and go home

Stopping to help is the clue: the story ends with Teacher Yasmin calling Amal the true winner because she was kind.

the text it is asked about
It was sports day at Amal's school. The sun was warm and the field was full of children. Everyone was ready to run, jump and hop.
“Drink your water,” said Teacher Yasmin. “Move your bodies and do your best. A strong body is a healthy body!”
Amal was in the green team with Nora, Sami and Leo. Theo was in the red team. Theo could run very fast, and he wanted everyone to know.
“My legs are the fastest legs in the school,” said Theo. He waved his arms and jumped up high. “The red team will win!”
“Do not worry,” Nora said to her team. She nodded her head. “We will run together and do our best.”
The first race was the hopping race. The children lined up. Teacher Yasmin raised her hand.
“Ready, steady, go!” she called.
The children hopped on one foot across the field. Leo was hopping fast, but he wobbled and nearly fell. “Keep going, Leo!” shouted Amal, clapping her hands. Leo did not give up. He hopped all the way to the line.
Next came the big relay race. In a relay, the runners take turns. Each runner passes a stick to the next one. Amal was the last runner for the green team.
“Ready, steady, go!” called Teacher Yasmin again.
Nora ran fast. Sami ran faster. Then Leo passed the stick to Amal. But Amal's tummy felt full of butterflies. Her legs felt slow.
“I am running as fast as I can,” Amal puffed. “But Theo is winning!”
Theo was far in front. He was busy waving to the crowd. He did not look at his feet. Suddenly he tripped and fell onto the grass. His stick rolled away.
Amal ran past him. Her team was cheering. “Go, Amal, go!” they shouted, clapping and jumping. Amal reached the line first. The green team had won!
Everyone cheered and waved. But Amal looked back. Theo was still sitting on the grass. He was rubbing his knee, and he looked very sad.
Amal did not jump for joy. She walked back to Theo and put out her hand.
“Are you hurt?” she asked kindly.
“Only a little,” said Theo. “But I lost. And I said so many silly things.”
Amal smiled. “You are still the fastest runner in the school,” she said. “Come on. Stand up.”
She helped Theo stand. They walked back together, and all the children clapped for them.
Teacher Yasmin nodded and smiled. “Well done, everyone,” she said. “Amal, you ran your best and you never gave up. And you were kind to Theo. That is the true winner.”
That evening, Amal told her mother about the big race. “I won,” she said, “but the best part was helping Theo. Next time, we will be on the same team!”
Her mother hugged her. “You have a fast body and a kind heart,” she said. “Now drink your water and rest. Tomorrow is another day to move and play.”

eng-g02-t01-u03-quiz01-q01 · unit 3 · Multiple choice · Ready, Steady, Go! checkpoint

Which word means the part at the end of your arm, with fingers?

hand  |  water  |  sleep  |  energy

A hand sits at the end of each arm. Water, sleep and energy are health words, not body parts.

eng-g02-t01-u03-quiz01-q02 · unit 3 · Multiple choice · Ready, Steady, Go! checkpoint

Sami holds his pencil with his ____s.

toe  |  finger  |  water  |  turn

We hold a pencil between our fingers and thumb. Toes are on your feet, and water and turn are not body parts of the hand.

eng-g02-t01-u03-quiz01-q03 · unit 3 · Multiple choice · Ready, Steady, Go! checkpoint

Which word means to jump up and down on one foot?

clap  |  water  |  hop  |  head

Hopping uses one foot only. Clapping uses hands, water is a drink, and head is a body part.

eng-g02-t01-u03-quiz01-q04 · unit 3 · Multiple choice · Ready, Steady, Go! checkpoint

Which sentence uses the present continuous correctly?

She jumping on the grass.  |  She are jumping on the grass.  |  She am jumping on the grass.  |  She is jumping on the grass.

She takes the helping word is. Am belongs with I, and are belongs with you, we and they.

eng-g02-t01-u03-quiz01-q05 · unit 3 · Multiple choice · Ready, Steady, Go! checkpoint

Which is the correct -ing form of the verb hop?

hoping  |  hopping  |  hopps  |  hopped

Hop is a short word with one vowel, so the p doubles before -ing. Hoping comes from hope, and hopped is a past form.

eng-g02-t01-u03-quiz01-q06 · unit 3 · Multiple choice · Ready, Steady, Go! checkpoint

Which of these sentences is a command?

Touch your toes.  |  My toes are cold.  |  Are your toes cold?  |  I am touching my toes.

A command starts with the action word and tells someone to do something. The others describe, ask or report.

eng-g02-t01-u03-quiz01-q07 · unit 3 · Multiple choice · Ready, Steady, Go! checkpoint

Which of these helps your body stay healthy and strong?

Watching television all day  |  Sitting still for many hours  |  Staying awake all night  |  Drinking water every day

Water helps your body work well. The other three choices keep your body still or tired.

eng-g02-t01-u03-quiz01-q08 · unit 3 · Multiple choice · Ready, Steady, Go! checkpoint

In the poem “Reach for the Sky!”, where do you put your finger?

On your knee  |  On your ear  |  On your nose  |  On your elbow

Line three of the poem says, “Put your finger on your nose.” The other body parts are not in the poem.

eng-g02-t01-u03-quiz01-q09 · unit 3 · Multiple choice · Ready, Steady, Go! checkpoint

In “The Big Race”, why did Theo fall over?

He was waving to the crowd and did not watch his feet.  |  He tripped over a big bag that somebody had left on the grass.  |  He stopped in the middle of the race to drink his water.  |  He was helping Amal to stand up again after her fall.

The story says Theo was so busy waving that he did not look at his feet, and then he tripped.

eng-g02-t01-u03-quiz01-q10 · unit 3 · Multiple choice · Ready, Steady, Go! checkpoint

What does the word energy mean?

One of the small parts at the end of your foot.  |  To move your head up and down to say yes.  |  The power you get from food and rest to play and learn.  |  To stretch your arm out to something.

Energy is what food and rest give you. The other three options describe a toe, nodding and reaching.

Unit 4 — The Big Sky

eng-g02-t02-u04-quiz01-q01 · unit 4 · Multiple choice · The Big Sky checkpoint

What does the word shadow mean?

The time when the sun goes down in the evening.  |  White or grey shapes in the sky made of tiny drops of water.  |  A dark shape made when something blocks the light.  |  The brightness that lets us see things.

A shadow needs something solid to stop the light. The other three choices describe sunset, clouds and light.

eng-g02-t02-u04-quiz01-q02 · unit 4 · Multiple choice · The Big Sky checkpoint

Which of these gives Earth its light and heat in the day?

the sun  |  the evening  |  a raincoat  |  grey

The sun is the star closest to us, and it gives Earth both light and heat. Evening is a time, a raincoat is clothing, and grey is a colour.

eng-g02-t02-u04-quiz01-q03 · unit 4 · Multiple choice · The Big Sky checkpoint

Which of these is the time when your part of Earth faces away from the sun?

a cloud  |  night  |  a source  |  windy

When your part of Earth has turned away from the sun, it is night. A cloud is a sky object, a source is where something comes from, and windy describes the weather.

eng-g02-t02-u04-quiz01-q04 · unit 4 · Multiple choice · The Big Sky checkpoint

The sun is a ____ of light and heat.

shadow  |  midday  |  cloud  |  source

A source is the place something comes from, so the sun is our biggest source of light. A shadow is dark, midday is a time, and a cloud blocks the light.

eng-g02-t02-u04-quiz01-q05 · unit 4 · Multiple choice · The Big Sky checkpoint

Which sentence uses a describing word correctly?

The sky is colour blue.  |  The sky is blue.  |  The sky blue is.  |  The sky is the blue.

The describing word blue comes straight after is. The word colour is not needed, and the other two sentences are in the wrong order.

eng-g02-t02-u04-quiz01-q06 · unit 4 · Multiple choice · The Big Sky checkpoint

Which sentence is written correctly in the present simple?

The sun rise in the east.  |  The sun rises in the east.  |  The sun rising in the east.  |  The sun to rise in the east.

The sun is an “it”, so its verb takes -s. The other three sentences have the wrong verb form.

eng-g02-t02-u04-quiz01-q07 · unit 4 · Multiple choice · The Big Sky checkpoint

Yesterday I ____ at the moon for a long time.

look  |  looks  |  looked  |  looking

Yesterday tells us the action is already finished, so the verb adds -ed. Look and looks are present forms, and looking needs a helping word.

eng-g02-t02-u04-quiz01-q08 · unit 4 · Multiple choice · The Big Sky checkpoint

The sky is full of grey clouds and we cannot see the sun. What is the weather like?

sunny  |  orange  |  midday  |  cloudy

Cloudy means many clouds cover the sky and hide the sun. Orange is a colour, midday is a time, and sunny is the opposite of what the question describes.

eng-g02-t02-u04-quiz01-q09 · unit 4 · Multiple choice · The Big Sky checkpoint

The compound word sunrise is made from two little words. Which two?

sun + rise  |  sea + side  |  rain + coat  |  moon + light

Sunrise splits into sun and rise. The other three joins make seaside, raincoat and moonlight.

eng-g02-t02-u04-quiz01-q10 · unit 4 · Multiple choice · The Big Sky checkpoint

At midday the sun is high above you. What is your shadow like?

long and thin, stretching across the ground  |  orange and bright, like an evening sky  |  short, right under your feet  |  wet and cold, like a rain cloud

A high sun makes a short shadow that hides under your feet. Long shadows belong to the morning and the evening, and shadows are never orange or wet.

Unit 5 — Let's Measure

eng-g02-t02-u05-quiz01-q01 · unit 5 · Multiple choice · Let's Measure checkpoint

How many tens are there in one hundred?

five  |  ten  |  twenty  |  one hundred

One hundred is made of ten groups of ten, so the answer is ten. Five, twenty and one hundred are all real numbers, but none of them counts the tens.

eng-g02-t02-u05-quiz01-q02 · unit 5 · Multiple choice · Let's Measure checkpoint

Which number word comes straight after seventy when you count in tens?

sixty  |  seventeen  |  ninety  |  eighty

Counting in tens runs seventy, eighty, ninety. Sixty comes earlier, seventeen is not a ten, and ninety comes one step later.

eng-g02-t02-u05-quiz01-q03 · unit 5 · Multiple choice · Let's Measure checkpoint

Which shape has no corners and no straight sides?

circle  |  square  |  triangle  |  rectangle

A circle is completely round. The other three shapes are all built from straight sides that meet at corners.

eng-g02-t02-u05-quiz01-q04 · unit 5 · Multiple choice · Let's Measure checkpoint

How many sides does a triangle have?

one  |  two  |  three  |  four

A triangle has three straight sides and three corners. A square and a rectangle each have four sides instead.

eng-g02-t02-u05-quiz01-q05 · unit 5 · Multiple choice · Let's Measure checkpoint

Look at this pattern: circle, square, triangle, circle, square, triangle. Which shape comes next?

square  |  circle  |  triangle  |  heart

The three shapes repeat in the same order, so after triangle the pattern begins again with circle.

eng-g02-t02-u05-quiz01-q06 · unit 5 · Multiple choice · Let's Measure checkpoint

How many centimetres are there in one metre?

ten  |  fifty  |  ninety  |  one hundred

Teacher Yasmin tells the class that one metre is always one hundred centimetres long.

eng-g02-t02-u05-quiz01-q07 · unit 5 · Multiple choice · Let's Measure checkpoint

Which sentence compares the two animals correctly?

A snake is longer than a worm.  |  A snake is more long than a worm.  |  A snake is long than a worm.  |  A snake is longest than a worm.

Short describing words take -er rather than more, and a comparison of two things always needs than after the -er word.

eng-g02-t02-u05-quiz01-q08 · unit 5 · Multiple choice · Let's Measure checkpoint

Which sentence uses is or are correctly?

There is ten circles on the chart.  |  There are one clock on the wall.  |  There are ten circles on the chart.  |  There is many circles on the chart.

Ten is more than one, so the sentence needs are. There is only fits a single thing, such as one clock.

eng-g02-t02-u05-quiz01-q09 · unit 5 · Multiple choice · Let's Measure checkpoint

Choose the correct word: “I have ______ hands.”

too  |  two  |  to  |  ate

Two is the number 2. Too means also, to is a joining word, and ate is the past form of eat.

eng-g02-t02-u05-quiz01-q10 · unit 5 · Multiple choice · Let's Measure checkpoint

What does the word weight mean?

How long something is from end to end.  |  A design that repeats again and again.  |  A flat tool for drawing straight lines.  |  How heavy or how light something is.

Weight tells us how heavy something feels. The other three options describe length, a pattern and a ruler.

Unit 6 — All About Bugs

eng-g02-t02-u06-quiz01-q01 · unit 6 · Multiple choice · All About Bugs checkpoint

Which of these is a small animal with eight legs that spins a web?

cricket  |  nectar  |  spider  |  anthill

A spider has eight legs and spins a web. A cricket is a six-legged insect, nectar is a sweet drink and an anthill is a home.

eng-g02-t02-u06-quiz01-q02 · unit 6 · Multiple choice · All About Bugs checkpoint

Which insect collects nectar from flowers and makes honey?

web  |  bee  |  above  |  crawl

A bee collects nectar and makes honey. A web is what a spider spins, above is a position word and crawl is an action word.

eng-g02-t02-u06-quiz01-q03 · unit 6 · Multiple choice · All About Bugs checkpoint

How many legs does every insect have?

two  |  eight  |  six  |  none

All insects have six legs. Two legs belong to a bird, eight legs belong to a spider, and a worm is the creature with no legs.

eng-g02-t02-u06-quiz01-q04 · unit 6 · Multiple choice · All About Bugs checkpoint

Why is a spider not an insect?

It has eight legs, and insects have six.  |  It has wings, and insects have no wings.  |  It lives in the soil, and insects live in trees.  |  It makes honey, and insects do not make honey.

Counting the legs is the test. Insects have six legs, so a spider with eight legs belongs to a different family of animals.

eng-g02-t02-u06-quiz01-q05 · unit 6 · Multiple choice · All About Bugs checkpoint

A butterfly is sitting and touching the top of a flower. Which position word is correct?

on  |  under  |  between  |  above

On means touching the top of something. Under means below it, between needs two things, and above means higher up without touching.

eng-g02-t02-u06-quiz01-q06 · unit 6 · Multiple choice · All About Bugs checkpoint

Which sentence tells you that the ant has gone inside its home?

The ant is on the anthill.  |  The ant is above the anthill.  |  The ant is in the anthill.  |  The ant is in front of the anthill.

In means inside. On means touching the top, above means higher up, and in front of means just before it.

eng-g02-t02-u06-quiz01-q07 · unit 6 · Multiple choice · All About Bugs checkpoint

Which sentence uses the simple present tense correctly?

A spider spin a web.  |  A spider spins a web.  |  A spider spinning a web.  |  A spiders spin a web.

There is only one spider, so the verb takes an s. The other sentences leave the s off, have no helping word, or add the s to the naming word instead.

eng-g02-t02-u06-quiz01-q08 · unit 6 · Multiple choice · All About Bugs checkpoint

Choose the correct verb for this fact: “Bees ______ honey.”

making  |  makes  |  made  |  make

Bees is more than one, so the verb stays plain. Makes belongs with one bee, made is the past, and making needs a helping word.

eng-g02-t02-u06-quiz01-q09 · unit 6 · Multiple choice · All About Bugs checkpoint

Which question asks about the place where spiders live?

What do spiders eat?  |  Where do spiders live?  |  How do spiders spin a web?  |  Why do spiders hide?

Where always asks about a place. What asks about a thing, How asks about a way of doing something, and Why asks for a reason.

eng-g02-t02-u06-quiz01-q10 · unit 6 · Multiple choice · All About Bugs checkpoint

What does the word antennae mean?

The net of thin silk that a spider spins to catch insects.  |  The home that ants build under the ground.  |  The body parts that some insects use for flying.  |  The two thin parts on an insect's head that help it feel and smell.

Antennae sit on the head and help an insect feel and smell. The other three options describe a web, an anthill and wings.

Unit 7 — The World Around Us

eng-g02-t03-u07-quiz01-q01 · unit 7 · Multiple choice · The World Around Us checkpoint

Which word means rubbish that people drop on the ground in public places?

sunshine  |  litter  |  oxygen  |  branch

Litter is dropped rubbish. Sunshine is light from the sun, oxygen is a gas, and a branch is part of a tree.

eng-g02-t03-u07-quiz01-q02 · unit 7 · Multiple choice · The World Around Us checkpoint

What does recycling do?

It waters a plant so that it grows.  |  It counts the birds in a garden.  |  It measures how tall a tree is.  |  It turns used things into something new.

Recycling makes used paper, glass or plastic into something new to use again. The other three describe completely different jobs.

eng-g02-t03-u07-quiz01-q03 · unit 7 · Multiple choice · The World Around Us checkpoint

Which of these makes food for a plant using sunlight?

The green leaves  |  The brown soil  |  The clean air  |  The falling rain

Leaves use sunlight to make the plant's food. Soil, air and rain are around the plant, but they are not parts of it.

eng-g02-t03-u07-quiz01-q04 · unit 7 · Cloze · The World Around Us checkpoint

Amal put the tiny ______ into the soil, and months later they grew into trees.

buttons  |  shoes  |  seeds  |  roads

Seeds are the tiny plant parts that grow into new plants. Buttons, shoes and roads never grow.

eng-g02-t03-u07-quiz01-q05 · unit 7 · Multiple choice · The World Around Us checkpoint

Amal is holding two small seeds in her hand. Which sentence is correct?

This seeds in my hand are small.  |  These seeds in my hand are small.  |  Those seeds in my hand are small.  |  That seeds in my hand are small.

The seeds are near her hand and there is more than one of them, so the pointing word is these.

eng-g02-t03-u07-quiz01-q06 · unit 7 · Multiple choice · The World Around Us checkpoint

Which of these actions keeps our environment clean and safe for animals?

Dropping a bottle on the path  |  Leaving the tap running all day  |  Burning rubbish beside the road  |  Picking up litter in the park

Picking up litter keeps parks and streets safe for animals. The other three choices leave harm behind them.

eng-g02-t03-u07-quiz01-q07 · unit 7 · Multiple choice · The World Around Us checkpoint

What does the flower of a plant make?

New seeds  |  Fresh soil  |  Cold rain  |  Bright sunlight

A flower makes the seeds that grow into new plants. Soil, rain and sunlight are not made by any flower.

eng-g02-t03-u07-quiz01-q08 · unit 7 · Multiple choice · The World Around Us checkpoint

According to “Plants and Clean Air”, which gas do plants give out?

Carbon dioxide  |  Smoke  |  Oxygen  |  Steam

Plants take in carbon dioxide and give out oxygen, which is the gas that we breathe.

eng-g02-t03-u07-quiz01-q09 · unit 7 · Cloze · The World Around Us checkpoint

Look at the picture: Nora ______ the flowers right now.

watering  |  are watering  |  is watering  |  am watering

Nora is one person, so she takes is, and the verb keeps its -ing ending.

eng-g02-t03-u07-quiz01-q10 · unit 7 · Multiple choice · The World Around Us checkpoint

Which sentence uses the word appreciate correctly?

I appreciate for the tall trees.  |  I appreciate the tall trees.  |  I am appreciate the tall trees.  |  I appreciate to the tall trees.

Appreciate goes straight to the thing, with no for, no to and no extra helping word.

Unit 8 — Home, Sweet Home

eng-g02-t03-u08-quiz01-q01 · unit 8 · Multiple choice · Home, Sweet Home checkpoint

Which word means a home where bees live and make honey?

hive  |  broom  |  ladder  |  wardrobe

Bees build a hive and store their honey inside it. A broom, a ladder and a wardrobe are things at home, not animal homes.

eng-g02-t03-u08-quiz01-q02 · unit 8 · Multiple choice · Home, Sweet Home checkpoint

In “Different Kinds of Homes”, what are adobe houses built from?

Glass and steel  |  Mud and dry grass  |  Wood and rope  |  Brick and paint

The text says people build adobe houses from mud and dry grass, and that the thick walls keep them cool.

eng-g02-t03-u08-quiz01-q03 · unit 8 · Multiple choice · Home, Sweet Home checkpoint

Which of these things would you find in a bathroom?

sofa  |  nest  |  sink  |  ladder

A bathroom has a sink and a toilet for washing. A sofa belongs in the living room, a nest is a bird's home, and a ladder is not a bathroom thing.

eng-g02-t03-u08-quiz01-q04 · unit 8 · Cloze · Home, Sweet Home checkpoint

The lamp is ____ the two books.

above  |  between  |  under  |  in

Between means in the middle of two things, so the lamp has one book on each side of it.

eng-g02-t03-u08-quiz01-q05 · unit 8 · Multiple choice · Home, Sweet Home checkpoint

Which sentence is written correctly?

There is two chairs in the kitchen.  |  There are two chairs in the kitchen.  |  There am two chairs in the kitchen.  |  There be two chairs in the kitchen.

Two chairs is more than one thing, so the sentence needs there are.

eng-g02-t03-u08-quiz01-q06 · unit 8 · Multiple choice · Home, Sweet Home checkpoint

Which sentence talks about the future in the right way?

I will to sweep the floor.  |  I will sweeping the floor.  |  I will sweep the floor.  |  I sweep will the floor.

After will we use the plain verb, with no to and no -ing, and will always comes before the verb.

eng-g02-t03-u08-quiz01-q07 · unit 8 · Multiple choice · Home, Sweet Home checkpoint

In which room do people cook the family meal?

The bedroom  |  The bathroom  |  The kitchen  |  The living room

The kitchen is the room where food is cooked. People sleep in the bedroom, wash in the bathroom and rest in the living room.

eng-g02-t03-u08-quiz01-q08 · unit 8 · Multiple choice · Home, Sweet Home checkpoint

In “Helping Hands at Home”, what surprise had Father built in the garden?

A tree house  |  A stone wall  |  A flower bed  |  A new kitchen

Father built a tree house under the big tree, with walls, a small roof and a tiny window.

eng-g02-t03-u08-quiz01-q09 · unit 8 · Multiple choice · Home, Sweet Home checkpoint

What does the word rug mean?

A soft mat that covers part of the floor.  |  A basin where you wash with water.  |  A long, soft seat for two or three people.  |  An opening in a wall with glass that lets light in.

A rug lies on the floor. The other three options describe a sink, a sofa and a window.

eng-g02-t03-u08-quiz01-q10 · unit 8 · Multiple choice · Home, Sweet Home checkpoint

Which job means putting the plates and cups ready for a meal?

sweep the floor  |  wash the dishes  |  make my bed  |  set the table

Setting the table means laying out the plates, cups and spoons before people eat.

Unit 9 — Let's Explore the City!

eng-g02-t03-u09-guideq1 · unit 9 · Short answer · Using the City Guide

Which part of a guide book lists small things in alphabetical order?

The index  |  The contents page  |  The glossary

The index is at the back and lists small things in alphabetical order, so you can look up one word.

the text it is asked about
A guide book is made to be searched, not read from front to back.

Three parts help you find things fast.

The contents page is at the front. It lists the big sections in the order they
appear, with the page each one starts on.

The index is at the back. It lists small things in alphabetical order, so you can
look up one word.

The glossary explains hard words.

Contents

Getting around ....... page 2

Places to visit ....... page 6

Eating out ....... page 11

Index

aquarium, 7

bus station, 3

library, 8

underground, 4

You want to know when the aquarium opens. The index says aquarium is on page 7,
so you turn to page 7. You do not read pages 2 to 6 first.

You want to plan a whole day of travel. That is a big section, so you use the
contents page instead, and turn to Getting around on page 2.

eng-g02-t03-u09-guideq2 · unit 9 · Short answer · Using the City Guide

The index says aquarium, 7. Which page do you turn to?

Page 7  |  Page 2  |  Page 11

An index entry gives the page the thing is on, so aquarium, 7 sends you straight to page 7.

eng-g02-t03-u09-guideq3 · unit 9 · Short answer · Using the City Guide

You want to plan a whole day of travel. Which part do you use, and why?

The contents page, because travel is a big section  |  The index, because it is alphabetical  |  The glossary, because it explains words

The contents page lists the big sections, so a whole-day plan is found there; the index is for looking up one small thing.

underground, 4

You want to know when the aquarium opens. The index says aquarium is on page 7,
so you turn to page 7. You do not read pages 2 to 6 first.

You want to plan a whole day of travel. That is a big section, so you use the
contents page instead, and turn to Getting around on page 2.

eng-g02-t03-u09-quiz01-q01 · unit 9 · Multiple choice · Let's Explore the City! checkpoint

What does the word library mean?

A boat that carries people and cars across water.  |  A building where you can read books and borrow them to take home.  |  All the cars, buses and lorries moving on the roads.  |  A sea animal with eight long arms and no bones at all.

A library is full of books to read and to borrow. The other three options describe a ferry, traffic and an octopus.

eng-g02-t03-u09-quiz01-q02 · unit 9 · Multiple choice · Let's Explore the City! checkpoint

Which word means a large building with many different shops inside it?

shopping centre  |  penguin  |  helicopter  |  directions

A shopping centre holds many shops under one roof. A penguin is a bird, a helicopter flies, and directions are words that tell you the way.

eng-g02-t03-u09-quiz01-q03 · unit 9 · Multiple choice · Let's Explore the City! checkpoint

Which of these travels below the city streets?

the ferry  |  the Ferris wheel  |  the helicopter  |  the underground

The underground runs through tunnels below the streets. The ferry travels on water, the Ferris wheel turns in a park, and the helicopter flies above the city.

eng-g02-t03-u09-quiz01-q04 · unit 9 · Cloze · Let's Explore the City! checkpoint

We took the ____ across the river to the other side.

market  |  ferry  |  library  |  shark

A ferry is the boat that carries people across water. A market and a library are buildings, and a shark is a sea animal.

eng-g02-t03-u09-quiz01-q05 · unit 9 · Multiple choice · Let's Explore the City! checkpoint

Which spelling is correct?

Ferriss weel  |  Feris wheal  |  Ferrys wheel  |  Ferris wheel

Ferris wheel has a double r, a single s, and wheel is spelled with wh and a double e.

eng-g02-t03-u09-quiz01-q06 · unit 9 · Multiple choice · Let's Explore the City! checkpoint

How many arms does an octopus have?

Two  |  Six  |  Eight  |  Twelve

Reading 2 and the library book in the story both say that an octopus has eight arms.

eng-g02-t03-u09-quiz01-q07 · unit 9 · Multiple choice · Let's Explore the City! checkpoint

Which sentence uses the present simple correctly?

The ferry take people across the river.  |  The ferry takes people across the river.  |  The ferry taking people across the river.  |  The ferry taked people across the river.

One ferry is an it, so the verb needs the -s ending: takes. The other three leave out the -s, use an -ing form, or invent a past form.

eng-g02-t03-u09-quiz01-q08 · unit 9 · Multiple choice · Let's Explore the City! checkpoint

Which sentence talks about the future?

Tomorrow we will ride the ferry.  |  Yesterday we went to the aquarium.  |  We ate fruit at the market.  |  Last week we saw the sharks.

Tomorrow and will both point forward. The other three sentences use the past verbs went, ate and saw.

eng-g02-t03-u09-quiz01-q09 · unit 9 · Multiple choice · Let's Explore the City! checkpoint

Your friend says, “I like the library.” Which reply agrees politely?

I don't.  |  I no like it.  |  Why do you?  |  I do too!

“I do too!” agrees. “I don't.” disagrees, “I no like it.” is not correct English, and “Why do you?” asks a question instead of giving an opinion.

eng-g02-t03-u09-quiz01-q10 · unit 9 · Multiple choice · Let's Explore the City! checkpoint

Adam told the lost man to walk straight ahead. What does straight ahead mean?

Turn left at the corner.  |  Go back the way you came.  |  Go forward without turning.  |  Cross the road at the crossing.

Straight ahead means keep going forward and do not turn to the left or the right.

Unit 10 — My English World: Grade 2 Capstone

eng-g02-t03-u10-predq1 · unit 10 · Short answer · Amal's English Year

Amal's English Year begins with her shy on the first day. What did that tell you about what the end of the year would show?

That she would grow more confident by the end  |  That she would stay quiet all year  |  That she would move to a new school

A story that starts with a shy first day is setting up a change; the end shows Amal presenting her own book to the class.

the text it is asked about
Amal opened her English folder on the last Monday of Year 2. At the front she found her welcome card from Unit 1. Her name was written in careful blue letters. Behind it lay a poem about her neighbour Theo. Next to the poem was a drawing of Leila the firefighter.

Amal turned another page. She found her shadow chart from the big sky unit. She found a measuring table and a bug fact file. She found her garden promise as well. Last of all she found her city map from Unit 9.

Teacher Yasmin smiled at the class. “This term you will build one last project,” she said. “It is called My English World.” Every child had to choose six pages from at least four different units.

Amal read her old work again. She fixed one sentence on her helpers page. She had written “The firefighters is working.” Now she wrote “The firefighters are working.” She added two describing words to her sky page. Unit 4 had taught her about adjectives.

Then Amal made her new booklet. Page one showed a butterfly with its wings and antennae. Page two showed a helpful neighbour. Page three showed the parts of the body. Page four showed the sun and a long shadow. Page five showed the trees she helped to plant. Page six showed the library in the city.

On showcase day Amal stood beside her table. Her hands felt cold, so she breathed slowly. “Welcome to my English world,” she said. Nora and Leo listened, and Grandma Hana clapped.

At the end Amal wrote one last reflection. “I can read, write and speak more clearly now,” she wrote. “Next year I want to read a long chapter book.” She closed her folder and felt proud.

eng-g02-t03-u10-quiz01-q01 · unit 10 · Multiple choice · Grade 2 English capstone checkpoint

This is Amal. ______ is presenting her booklet to the class.

He  |  She  |  They  |  We

Amal is one girl, so we use she. Unit 1 taught this pronoun.

eng-g02-t03-u10-quiz01-q02 · unit 10 · Multiple choice · Grade 2 English capstone checkpoint

The firefighters ______ helping on Warta Street.

is  |  am  |  was  |  are

Firefighters is more than one person, so the verb is are. This is the Unit 2 pattern.

eng-g02-t03-u10-quiz01-q03 · unit 10 · Multiple choice · Grade 2 English capstone checkpoint

Which sentence is a command?

Point at page four.  |  Amal points at page four.  |  She is pointing at page four.  |  Amal will point at page four.

A command starts with the plain verb and tells somebody what to do, as in Unit 3.

eng-g02-t03-u10-quiz01-q04 · unit 10 · Multiple choice · Grade 2 English capstone checkpoint

Which sentence compares two things correctly?

The metre is more long than the centimetre.  |  The metre is longest than the centimetre.  |  The metre is longer than the centimetre.  |  The metre long than the centimetre.

Short describing words take -er plus than, which Unit 5 taught for measuring.

eng-g02-t03-u10-quiz01-q05 · unit 10 · Multiple choice · Grade 2 English capstone checkpoint

Which word tells you a position?

quickly  |  under  |  butterfly  |  shouted

Under is a position word from Unit 6. Quickly tells how, butterfly names an insect and shouted is an action.

eng-g02-t03-u10-quiz01-q06 · unit 10 · Multiple choice · Grade 2 English capstone checkpoint

Which word is a describing word?

jump  |  library  |  quickly  |  bright

Bright describes the sky, as in Unit 4. Jump is an action, library names a place and quickly tells how.

eng-g02-t03-u10-quiz01-q07 · unit 10 · Multiple choice · Grade 2 English capstone checkpoint

Which sentence tells you about the past?

We planted a tree last term.  |  We are planting a tree today.  |  We will plant a tree in Year 3.  |  We plant trees every year.

Planted ends in -ed and last term is finished, so this is the past from Unit 4.

eng-g02-t03-u10-quiz01-q08 · unit 10 · Multiple choice · Grade 2 English capstone checkpoint

There ______ three pictures beside the booklet.

is  |  am  |  are  |  was

Three pictures are more than one, so we use There are, as Unit 8 taught.

eng-g02-t03-u10-quiz01-q09 · unit 10 · Multiple choice · Grade 2 English capstone checkpoint

Which word means a large building with many different shops inside it?

library  |  shopping centre  |  flat  |  hut

A library holds books, a flat is rooms to live in and a hut is a small home. Only the shopping centre holds many shops.

eng-g02-t03-u10-quiz01-q10 · unit 10 · Multiple choice · Grade 2 English capstone checkpoint

Your partner's page has good ideas but no full stops. What is the kindest and most useful thing to say?

This page is wrong.  |  I do not like it.  |  Change everything again.  |  Your ideas are clear, so add full stops at the end.

Good feedback names something that works and gives one clear next step.
